      LICHTENBERG's eta, ATTRACTOR's (a,b,c,d), SOFTCELL's softness t,
      EINSTEIN/SPECTRE's recursion depth
- [ ] FLAME: a proper GPU parallel-reduction max (mip-chain or log-step
      ping-pong) for the log-tone-map's self-normalizing peak density,
      replacing this session's CPU gl.readPixels-and-scan every 15 frames
      — would remove the one remaining per-frame CPU/GPU sync point and
      scale past the current 384x384 accumulation resolution
- [ ] FLAME: post-transforms (a second affine applied AFTER the variation,
      per Draves' own full xform spec) — this session's xforms only use
      the pre-affine + variation half, the simpler of the two; adding
      post-transforms would unlock parameter sets that need the extra
      degree of freedom (many published .flame files rely on both)
- [ ] FLAME: blend multiple variations within a single xform by weight
      (Draves' spec allows an xform to sum 2+ variations), rather than
      this session's one-variation-per-xform simplification — would let
      one branch's own output continuously morph between two variation
      characters instead of only switching between branches
- [ ] FLAME: a HORSESHOE SPIRAL follow-up that pushes the spiral
      variation's share back up from this session's tuned-down 0.10
      weight now that the r-floor (0.12) and reseed bound (25.0) fixes
      exist — this session prioritized taming the singularity-driven
      noise it caused at higher weight over exploring how far its
      "feathery spike" character can go once properly bounded
- [ ] FLAME: a 3D IFS / fractal-flame mode (post-transforms and
      variations both generalize to 3 dimensions in Draves' own later
      work), echoing the standing "3D reaction-diffusion"/"3D
      Lichtenberg"/"3D attractor"/"3D soft-cell" backlog entries that
      never got built in this gallery for performance reasons — worth
      reconsidering here since, like a strange attractor or DBM growth, a
      3D chaos-game orbit is a sparse point cloud rather than a dense
      volumetric field
- [x] Cahn-Hilliard phase separation / spinodal decomposition — done in
      SPINODAL (session 17), the gallery's first fourth-order (conserved-
      order-parameter) PDE, distinct from every earlier reaction-diffusion
      piece's non-conserved activator/inhibitor dynamics
- [ ] SPINODAL: a semi-implicit (Eyre-splitting) time step instead of
      explicit Euler — this session's dt is bounded well under the
      biharmonic stability limit (dt*M*|8-64*kappa|<=2) purely because
      explicit stepping demands it; an implicit linear part would allow
      much larger dt (and either a denser grid or faster real-time
      coarsening) at real numerical-methods cost this session didn't
      attempt
- [ ] SPINODAL: a proper GPU parallel-reduction for the total-variation
      readout, replacing this session's CPU gl.readPixels-and-scan every
      12 frames — the same remaining per-frame CPU/GPU sync point FLAME's
      own backlog flags for its log-tone-map peak-density readback
- [ ] SPINODAL: a true nucleation-and-growth mode (composition OUTSIDE
      the spinodal region, |c0|>1/sqrt3, decomposing only via a
      stochastic Cahn-Hilliard-Cook noise term overcoming a real energy
      barrier) as an explicit contrast against every current mode's
      barrierless spinodal decomposition — not attempted this session,
      since it needs an added thermal-noise term the deterministic PDE
      here doesn't have, not just a different c0
- [ ] SPINODAL: an interactive kappa slider (continuous, visitor-
      controlled interfacial energy / characteristic wavelength) instead
      of only the 5 curated auto-cycled values — the same "let a visitor
      walk the parameter themselves" idea flagged for ISING's
      temperature, LICHTENBERG's eta, ATTRACTOR's (a,b,c,d), SOFTCELL's
      softness t, EINSTEIN/SPECTRE's recursion depth, and FLAME's own
      xform editor
- [ ] SPINODAL: a 3D mode (Cahn-Hilliard generalizes directly to a
      volumetric bicontinuous microstructure — real spinodal alloys and
      block copolymers separate in 3D), echoing the standing "3D
      reaction-diffusion"/"3D Lichtenberg"/"3D attractor"/"3D soft-
      cell"/"3D flame" backlog entries never built here for performance
      reasons — worth reconsidering since, like DBM growth or a strange
      attractor, a 3D bicontinuous interface is a comparatively sparse
      surface rather than a dense volumetric field
- [x] A raymarched/camera piece answering the standing "3D" backlog itch
      (SPINODAL, FLAME, ATTRACTOR, SOFTCELL, LICHTENBERG all flagged one
      as never built for performance reasons) — done in HORIZON
      (session 18), via a genuinely different technique (per-pixel
      geodesic integration, sparse per-ray cost) rather than a dense
      volumetric field sim, general relativity rather than another
      field/agent/lattice system
- [ ] HORIZON: verify actual frame rate on real GPU hardware — this
      session only measured headless SwiftShader (software, deliberately
      non-representative, under 2fps), unlike every other performance
      claim in this gallery which was measured on a real path; if real
      hardware turns out slower than expected, the first lever is
      MAXSTEPS (currently 110) and the internal resolution cap
      (currently 660px), both already tuned down once this session
- [ ] HORIZON: the Kerr metric (rotating black hole) — frame-dragging
      would split the photon-capture cross-section into prograde/
      retrograde halves and shift the ISCO inward/outward by spin,
      genuinely different geodesic equations (no longer reducible to a
      single planar orbit problem the way Schwarzschild's spherical
      symmetry allows) rather than a parameter change to this session's
      ODE — not attempted, out of scope after the reference research
- [ ] HORIZON: true relativistic Doppler/redshift colour-shifting of the
      disk material by its own local orbital velocity (this session only
      approximates directional beaming as a brightness multiplier, not an
      actual wavelength shift toward blue/red) — would need the disk
      material's exact local 4-velocity, not just a tangent direction
- [ ] HORIZON: a volumetric (non-razor-thin) accretion disk or torus,
      raymarched through actual optical depth rather than a single
      equatorial-plane crossing test — a genuinely harder rendering
      problem than the current thin-disk approximation, deliberately
      left as a simpler model this session
- [ ] HORIZON: an interactive camera-distance/FOV slider independent of
      the 5 curated auto-cycled modes, echoing the same "let a visitor
      walk the parameter themselves" idea flagged for ISING's
      temperature, LICHTENBERG's eta, ATTRACTOR's (a,b,c,d), SOFTCELL's
      softness t, EINSTEIN/SPECTRE's recursion depth, FLAME's xform
      editor, and SPINODAL's kappa — this session's drag-to-orbit
      interaction covers azimuth/inclination but not radius or FOV
- [ ] HORIZON: a naked-singularity or wormhole variant (Reissner-Nordström
      with Q>M removes the horizon entirely, or a Morris-Thorne throat)
      as an explicit contrast against every current mode's ordinary
      Schwarzschild horizon — needs a genuinely different metric/effective
      potential, not just a different camera, not attempted this session
- [ ] HORIZON: a proper adaptive step size (finer dφ specifically near the
      photon sphere, coarser far from it) instead of this session's fixed
      global DPHI plus local bisection-at-crossings — would let the photon
      ring resolve even more cleanly, or the same quality at a lower
      MAXSTEPS budget, at real numerical-methods cost this session's
      bisection fix sidestepped rather than solved head-on
- [x] Fluid dynamics / Navier-Stokes (Stam's "stable fluids") — done in
      EDDY (session 19), the gallery's first non-local-rule PDE (a global
      Poisson pressure-projection solve every frame, not a fixed local
      update) and first piece with velocity advecting itself
- [ ] EDDY: a separate, higher-resolution dye grid than the velocity/
      pressure solve grid (as most WebGL fluid demos do, e.g. Pavel
      Dobryakov's WebGL-Fluid-Simulation's SIM_RESOLUTION vs
      DYE_RESOLUTION) — this session shares one 320x160 grid for
      everything, simpler to implement correctly but coarser dye detail
      than the solve itself would allow
- [ ] EDDY: a proper implicit viscosity solve (diffuse velocity via its
      own small Jacobi/Poisson pass, Stam's original algorithm) instead
      of this session's single explicit blend-toward-neighbour-average —
      the explicit version is what this session's second real bug needed
      to control vorticity-confinement's grid-noise amplification, but a
      real implicit diffusion step would give an actual, tunable
      Reynolds-number-like dial instead of a fixed damping fraction
- [ ] EDDY: the Kerr-style "what's actually different" backlog itch this
      time is a rotating/spinning obstacle in KARMAN STREET (a rotating
      cylinder sheds asymmetrically and can suppress shedding entirely
      past a critical spin ratio, the Magnus-effect regime) — needs a
      no-slip tangential boundary condition at the solid, not just
      zeroing velocity there, not attempted this session
- [ ] EDDY: LEAPFROG's pairs currently fade within roughly half the
      mode's ~30s hold rather than completing many more visible cycles —
      the per-mode diffusion coefficient this session landed on
      (diffK=0.06) was tuned empirically via headless screenshots against
      the same instability the KARMAN-scale modes fight at diffK=0.16;
      a proper implicit-viscosity fix above would likely buy LEAPFROG
      meaningfully more honest lifetime without reopening the checkerboard
      instability a lower explicit diffK let back in during this session's
      own sweep
- [ ] EDDY: a 3D version (volumetric Navier-Stokes, likely raymarched
      density like a real smoke-sim renderer) — echoes the gallery's
      standing "3D" backlog itch (SPINODAL/FLAME/ATTRACTOR/SOFTCELL/
      LICHTENBERG all flagged one for performance reasons, HORIZON
      eventually answered it via a genuinely different technique) — a
      dense volumetric pressure solve is a much heavier lift than
      HORIZON's sparse per-ray cost, not attempted this session
- [ ] EDDY: an interactive Reynolds-number-style slider (once a real
      viscosity dial exists per the implicit-diffusion item above),
      echoing the same "let a visitor walk the parameter themselves" idea
      standing since ISING's temperature and carried through LICHTENBERG,
      ATTRACTOR, SOFTCELL, EINSTEIN/SPECTRE, FLAME, SPINODAL and HORIZON
- [ ] EDDY: true relativistic-style Doppler colour-shift is HORIZON's own
      backlog item, but EDDY has its own honest analogue never attempted —
      real Schlieren/shadowgraph-style refractive-index colouring of the
      flow (as real fluid-visualisation photography does) instead of the

      reason; resolving it properly would need either a still-better-adapted
      mesh (this session already moved from a uniform Cartesian grid to a
      polar r=w*u/(1-u) mesh once, catching a real drifting-away-from-1 bug
      in the process) or an analytic closed-form evaluation of the l=1
      solid-angle integral, neither attempted.
- [ ] SKYRMION: a fourth mode showing the spin-angular-momentum or magnetic-
      field skyrmion textures specifically (two of the paper's other three
      reported skyrmion types, alongside the Stokes/polarization one this
      session built) — would need the full vectorial field (see the gap two
      items above) to define honestly, not attempted this session.
- [ ] SKYRMION: POISSON mode's disc is circular only; the historical
      literature also covers diffraction around irregular/non-circular
      obstacles (where the on-axis bright-spot argument does NOT simply
      generalize by symmetry) — not attempted, would need abandoning the
      radially-symmetric Hankel-style reduction this session's disc integral
      relies on for a genuine 2D integral.
- [x] A piece rooted in botany rather than animal biology, physics, or pure
      mathematics — done in VENATION (session 31), leaf major veins as a
      live Voronoi diagram of the leaf's hydathode pores.
- [ ] VENATION: the real leaf's physical veins are fixed tissue laid down
      over developmental time and never rewired afterward, so the actual
      paper's own vein network only approximately matches the ideal Voronoi
      diagram (~73% of real vein-loops contain exactly one pore); this
      session's GROWTH mode instead fully recomputes the ideal diagram from
      scratch every frame as pores are added, an easier but less faithful
      mechanism — an incremental/"frozen-edge" Voronoi update (new pores
      only locally perturbing nearby existing veins, never globally
      relocating settled ones) would let a visitor see that ~73%-style
      approximation error emerge from the mechanism itself, not attempted
      this session.
- [ ] VENATION: the paper's own 34-leaf hydathode/vein dataset was not
      available to this session; every pore layout rendered is synthetic
      (Bridson blue-noise / jittered lattice), demonstrating the GEOMETRIC
      RULE the paper reports rather than reproducing its own measurements
      — digitizing even one real traced leaf (from the paper's own figures)
      would let a future session show the actual biological data obeying
      the rule, not just a synthetic stand-in.
- [ ] VENATION: real Pilea peperomioides leaves are peltate (the petiole
      attaches at the leaf's centre on the underside, not the edge), and
      real primary veins radiate from that attachment point across the
      whole lamina; this session's centre marker and faint radiating-spoke
      overlay are a cosmetic gesture at that detail, not a physically
      modeled primary-vein network distinct from the secondary/reticulate
      Voronoi-like veins the piece actually verifies.
- [ ] VENATION: COMPARE mode's single continuous "disorder" dial (a
      jittered-hexagonal-lattice jitter parameter pushed past 1) was
      verified this session to approach but not exactly reach the
      independent-random-point cell-area CV at its extreme (0.48 vs. 0.52,
      averaged) — a true crossfade into actual independent Poisson points
      at the dial's far end, rather than an ever-more-jittered lattice that
      never fully forgets its ancestry, was not attempted.
- [x] A piece that treats one of this gallery's own earlier pieces'
      generative structure as input to a new physical calculation, rather
      than starting a new subject from zero — done in DIFFRACT (session
      32), reusing SPECTRE's ported tile grammar as the point-scatterer set
      for a kinematic diffraction calculation.
- [ ] DIFFRACT: a genuinely VECTORIAL near-field or finite-aperture
      diffraction treatment (each tile as an extended aperture, not a
      collapsed point scatterer) would reproduce the paper's own measured
      peak intensities/envelope and its reported circular-polarization-
      dependent response — this session's scalar point-scatterer kinematic
      structure factor reproduces peak POSITIONS and the mirror-asymmetry
      signature only, the same near-field-vs-scalar gap SKYRMION's own
      header discloses for its POISSON mode, not attempted here either.
- [ ] DIFFRACT: the paper's own reported "perfect threefold rotational
      symmetry" of the diffraction pattern remains unverified — this
      session's first attempt (comparing point-sampled intensities against
      a 120-degree-rotated copy) was diagnosed as testing the wrong thing
      (narrow Bragg peaks need peak-to-peak matching, not point-sampled
      intensity comparison, to check rotational symmetry meaningfully) and
      was dropped rather than shipped; a peak-detection-then-match
      algorithm would need to find local maxima in the structure factor
      first, not attempted.
- [ ] DIFFRACT: the "spot size" aperture control approximates a real
      finite-illuminated-area effect but is not literally the paper's own
      published beam-width parameter (not extracted from the paper in
      numerical form this session); a version calibrated to the paper's
      actual reported spot sizes, if extractable from a fuller read of its
      methods, would let a visitor compare against the paper's own
      specific reported peak widths rather than only the qualitative trend.
- [ ] DIFFRACT: only the SPECTRE point set and a periodic triangular
      lattice are offered; a third comparison — the EINSTEIN "hat" tile's
      own point set, which needs a ~12.7% mix of reflected copies to tile
      and so is only PARTIALLY chiral — would show an intermediate mirror-
      asymmetry percentage between DIFFRACT's ~65% and LATTICE's ~0%, not
      attempted this session.
- [ ] The Compact Bonnet Pairs counterexample to Bonnet's ~150-year-old
      surface-rigidity theorem (Bobenko, Hoffmann & Sageman-Furnas,
      "Compact Bonnet Pairs: isometric tori with the same curvatures,"
      Publications Mathematiques de l'IHES 142, 2025/2026) remains
      unaddressed after FIVE consecutive sessions' web wanders (29-32) —
      this session went further than prior ones and actually read the
      paper's own construction (isothermic surfaces with one family of
      planar curvature lines, related to Bonnet pairs via a Christoffel
      transform, with explicit closing conditions via elliptic integrals)
      rather than only its abstract, and judged the numerical machinery
      (solving the closing conditions, tracing the resulting immersed tori)
      too deep relative to any implementation this gallery has attempted —
      a genuinely harder future-session undertaking than a typical "port a
      published construction" piece, not a matter of not having looked.
